What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I don’t want to make too much of it… maybe it’s nothing, but I came because sometimes the men over at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, they say things that feel like little messages meant for me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

That’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any problems with your memory lately?\n\nPatient: Memory? Well… I don’t think so, not in any big way. I mean, at my age I might misplace my glasses or walk into a room and forget what I came for, but I figure that happens to most folks, doesn’t it? I haven’t noticed anything too troublesome. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences started, have you generally tended to distrust other people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor… not as a rule. I mean, I’ve always tried to be careful with folks, same as anybody, but I wouldn’t say I went around distrusting people before all this. At least, not that I noticed. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: About how long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Oh… I’d say it’s been about a year and some months now. Maybe around a year and five months, if I’m counting right. It seemed to start after my close friend passed away. I didn’t think much of it at first, you know… grief can do funny things, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any brain or nervous system illness, such as a stroke, Parkinson’s disease, or seizures?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t believe I’ve been told I have anything like that. No stroke or Parkinson’s or seizures that I know of. I mean, doctors haven’t said that to me, anyway… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose some. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, doctor, because I still love my family very much. But a lot of days I just end up sitting at the kitchen table, not saying much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice will come and go, and I just… don’t feel like getting myself moving. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed most of the day, nearly every day. I have low spells, sure, but I try not to dwell on them. Some days are quieter than others. I’m not sure I’m answering that the right way, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually confused?\n\nPatient: Unusually confused… I don’t think so, no. I mean, at my age I’ll forget a word or walk into a room and wonder what I came for, but I don’t think I’ve been confused in a way that worried anybody. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, I don’t believe so, doctor. I haven’t seen anyone standing there or anything like that.\n\nSometimes, though… well, I don’t know if this counts. In the quiet afternoons, I’ll hear my old friend call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs, clear as if he were right there. But seeing things, no… not that I can say. Is that alright to mention?\n\nDoctor: During the times when these messages or voices happen, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say that, doctor. When those voices happen, I might feel a bit lonely or low, but… two whole weeks, most of the day nearly every day, down or empty? I don’t think I’ve noticed it like that. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences began, had you started any new medicine or begun drinking more alcohol or using any drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I haven’t noticed anything like starting a new pill or drinking more than usual before it began. And I don’t use drugs. At least… nothing comes to mind that would explain it that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any problems with your memory lately?\n\nPatient: Memory? Well… I don’t think so, not in any big way. I mean, at my age I might misplace my glasses or walk into a room and forget what I came for, but I figure that happens to most folks, doesn’t it? I haven’t noticed anything too troublesome. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences started, have you generally tended to distrust other people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor… not as a rule. I mean, I’ve always tried to be careful with folks, same as anybody, but I wouldn’t say I went around distrusting people before all this. At least, not that I noticed. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: About how long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Oh… I’d say it’s been about a year and some months now. Maybe around a year and five months, if I’m counting right. It seemed to start after my close friend passed away. I didn’t think much of it at first, you know… grief can do funny things, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any brain or nervous system illness, such as a stroke, Parkinson’s disease, or seizures?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t believe I’ve been told I have anything like that. No stroke or Parkinson’s or seizures that I know of. I mean, doctors haven’t said that to me, anyway… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose some. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, doctor, because I still love my family very much. But a lot of days I just end up sitting at the kitchen table, not saying much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice will come and go, and I just… don’t feel like getting myself moving. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed most of the day, nearly every day. I have low spells, sure, but I try not to dwell on them. Some days are quieter than others. I’m not sure I’m answering that the right way, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually confused?\n\nPatient: Unusually confused… I don’t think so, no. I mean, at my age I’ll forget a word or walk into a room and wonder what I came for, but I don’t think I’ve been confused in a way that worried anybody. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, I don’t believe so, doctor. I haven’t seen anyone standing there or anything like that.\n\nSometimes, though… well, I don’t know if this counts. In the quiet afternoons, I’ll hear my old friend call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs, clear as if he were right there. But seeing things, no… not that I can say. Is that alright to mention?\n\nDoctor: During the times when these messages or voices happen, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say that, doctor. When those voices happen, I might feel a bit lonely or low, but… two whole weeks, most of the day nearly every day, down or empty? I don’t think I’ve noticed it like that. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences began, had you started any new medicine or begun drinking more alcohol or using any drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I haven’t noticed anything like starting a new pill or drinking more than usual before it began. And I don’t use drugs. At least… nothing comes to mind that would explain it that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you seem less expressive or show less emotion on your face than you used to?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose a few folks have said I seem quieter, maybe not showing much on my face. I don’t mean to be cold or anything—I still love my family very much.\n\nIt’s just that some days I sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice comes and goes, and I just… don’t feel much like getting up and moving. I hope that makes sense, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical illness around the time this started, such as a serious infection, head injury, or another new health problem?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, not that I can think of. I mean, I’ve had the usual aches and things at my age, but I don’t recall any serious infection or head injury or new illness right around then. I hope I’m answering that right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I have low spells, sure, but I try not to dwell on them. Some days are quieter than others. I’m not sure I’m answering that the right way, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually confused?\n\nPatient: Unusually confused… I don’t think so, no. I mean, at my age I’ll forget a word or walk into a room and wonder what I came for, but I don’t think I’ve been confused in a way that worried anybody. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, I don’t believe so, doctor. I haven’t seen anyone standing there or anything like that.\n\nSometimes, though… well, I don’t know if this counts. In the quiet afternoons, I’ll hear my old friend call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs, clear as if he were right there. But seeing things, no… not that I can say. Is that alright to mention?\n\nDoctor: During the times when these messages or voices happen, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say that, doctor. When those voices happen, I might feel a bit lonely or low, but… two whole weeks, most of the day nearly every day, down or empty? I don’t think I’ve noticed it like that. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences began, had you started any new medicine or begun drinking more alcohol or using any drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I haven’t noticed anything like starting a new pill or drinking more than usual before it began. And I don’t use drugs. At least… nothing comes to mind that would explain it that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you seem less expressive or show less emotion on your face than you used to?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose a few folks have said I seem quieter, maybe not showing much on my face. I don’t mean to be cold or anything—I still love my family very much.\n\nIt’s just that some days I sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice comes and goes, and I just… don’t feel much like getting up and moving. I hope that makes sense, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical illness around the time this started, such as a serious infection, head injury, or another new health problem?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, not that I can think of. I mean, I’ve had the usual aches and things at my age, but I don’t recall any serious infection or head injury or new illness right around then. I hope I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Are the voice experiences only of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly… it’s my old friend who passed away. Some afternoons, when the house is real quiet, I’ll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs. I don’t mean to be cold or anything—I still love my family very much.\n\nIt’s just that some days I sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice comes and goes, and I just… don’t feel much like getting up and moving. I hope that makes sense, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical illness around the time this started, such as a serious infection, head injury, or another new health problem?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, not that I can think of. I mean, I’ve had the usual aches and things at my age, but I don’t recall any serious infection or head injury or new illness right around then. I hope I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Are the voice experiences only of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly… it’s my old friend who passed away. I haven’t seen anyone standing there or anything like that.\n\nSometimes, though… well, I don’t know if this counts. In the quiet afternoons, I’ll hear my old friend call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs, clear as if he were right there. But seeing things, no… not that I can say. Is that alright to mention?\n\nDoctor: During the times when these messages or voices happen, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say that, doctor. When those voices happen, I might feel a bit lonely or low, but… two whole weeks, most of the day nearly every day, down or empty? I don’t think I’ve noticed it like that. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences began, had you started any new medicine or begun drinking more alcohol or using any drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I haven’t noticed anything like starting a new pill or drinking more than usual before it began. And I don’t use drugs. At least… nothing comes to mind that would explain it that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you seem less expressive or show less emotion on your face than you used to?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose a few folks have said I seem quieter, maybe not showing much on my face. I don’t mean to be cold or anything—I still love my family very much.\n\nIt’s just that some days I sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice comes and goes, and I just… don’t feel much like getting up and moving. I hope that makes sense, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical illness around the time this started, such as a serious infection, head injury, or another new health problem?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, not that I can think of. I mean, I’ve had the usual aches and things at my age, but I don’t recall any serious infection or head injury or new illness right around then. I hope I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Are the voice experiences only of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly… it’s my old friend who passed away. Some afternoons, when the house is real quiet, I’ll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs. It sounds clear, like he’s standing right there.\n\nI know that may sound strange, doctor. I don’t want to make too much of it, but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say someone is out to hurt me exactly, doctor. I don’t want to sound foolish.\n\nBut I did come because sometimes the men at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, say things that feel like little messages meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe I shouldn’t make too much of it… but I feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if your thoughts were being put into your mind or taken out by someone else?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like somebody putting thoughts in my head or taking them out, doctor. Not that I can tell.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I was saying, sometimes the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer, say things that feel like little messages meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe she’s right, but it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. I hope that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I didn’t think much of it at first, you know… grief can do funny things, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any brain or nervous system illness, such as a stroke, Parkinson’s disease, or seizures?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t believe I’ve been told I have anything like that. No stroke or Parkinson’s or seizures that I know of. I mean, doctors haven’t said that to me, anyway… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose some. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, doctor, because I still love my family very much. But a lot of days I just end up sitting at the kitchen table, not saying much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice will come and go, and I just… don’t feel like getting myself moving. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed most of the day, nearly every day. I have low spells, sure, but I try not to dwell on them. Some days are quieter than others. I’m not sure I’m answering that the right way, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually confused?\n\nPatient: Unusually confused… I don’t think so, no. I mean, at my age I’ll forget a word or walk into a room and wonder what I came for, but I don’t think I’ve been confused in a way that worried anybody. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, I don’t believe so, doctor. I haven’t seen anyone standing there or anything like that.\n\nSometimes, though… well, I don’t know if this counts. In the quiet afternoons, I’ll hear my old friend call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs, clear as if he were right there. But seeing things, no… not that I can say. Is that alright to mention?\n\nDoctor: During the times when these messages or voices happen, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say that, doctor. When those voices happen, I might feel a bit lonely or low, but… two whole weeks, most of the day nearly every day, down or empty? I don’t think I’ve noticed it like that. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences began, had you started any new medicine or begun drinking more alcohol or using any drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I haven’t noticed anything like starting a new pill or drinking more than usual before it began. And I don’t use drugs. At least… nothing comes to mind that would explain it that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you seem less expressive or show less emotion on your face than you used to?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose a few folks have said I seem quieter, maybe not showing much on my face. I don’t mean to be cold or anything—I still love my family very much.\n\nIt’s just that some days I sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice comes and goes, and I just… don’t feel much like getting up and moving. I hope that makes sense, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical illness around the time this started, such as a serious infection, head injury, or another new health problem?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, not that I can think of. I mean, I’ve had the usual aches and things at my age, but I don’t recall any serious infection or head injury or new illness right around then. I hope I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Are the voice experiences only of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly… it’s my old friend who passed away. Some afternoons, when the house is real quiet, I’ll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs. It sounds clear, like he’s standing right there.\n\nI know that may sound strange, doctor. I don’t want to make too much of it, but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say someone is out to hurt me exactly, doctor. I don’t want to sound foolish.\n\nBut I did come because sometimes the men at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, say things that feel like little messages meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe I shouldn’t make too much of it… but I feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if your thoughts were being put into your mind or taken out by someone else?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like somebody putting thoughts in my head or taking them out, doctor. Not that I can tell.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I was saying, sometimes the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer, say things that feel like little messages meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe she’s right, but it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anything impossible is happening to you, like someone controlling you from a distance or using special powers on you?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I wouldn’t say somebody is controlling me from far away or using powers on me. That sounds… bigger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more like what I was trying to say before—the men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, they’ll say little things that feel meant for me. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF20.0, F22, F06.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, I don’t want to make too much of it… maybe it’s nothing, but I came because sometimes the men over at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, they say things that feel like little messages meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just regular talk, and maybe she’s right, but… I can’t shake that they’re warning me to be careful. Is that all right to tell you like that?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard a voice speaking when no one was actually there?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I have, yes. Not all the time, doctor, and I don’t want to sound foolish. Some afternoons, when the house is real quiet, I’ll hear my old friend call my name from the porch… or sometimes it’s like he’s humming an old choir song. It sounds clear, like he’s standing right there, but when I look, nobody’s there. That’s the kind of thing you mean, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any problems with your memory lately?\n\nPatient: Memory? Well… I don’t think so, not in any big way. I mean, at my age I might misplace my glasses or walk into a room and forget what I came for, but I figure that happens to most folks, doesn’t it? I haven’t noticed anything too troublesome. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences started, have you generally tended to distrust other people?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor… not as a rule. I mean, I’ve always tried to be careful with folks, same as anybody, but I wouldn’t say I went around distrusting people before all this. At least, not that I noticed. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: About how long have these experiences been going on?\n\nPatient: Oh… I’d say it’s been about a year and some months now. Maybe around a year and five months, if I’m counting right. It seemed to start after my close friend passed away. I didn’t think much of it at first, you know… grief can do funny things, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been diagnosed with any brain or nervous system illness, such as a stroke, Parkinson’s disease, or seizures?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t believe I’ve been told I have anything like that. No stroke or Parkinson’s or seizures that I know of. I mean, doctors haven’t said that to me, anyway… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose some. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, doctor, because I still love my family very much. But a lot of days I just end up sitting at the kitchen table, not saying much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice will come and go, and I just… don’t feel like getting myself moving. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it depressed most of the day, nearly every day. I have low spells, sure, but I try not to dwell on them. Some days are quieter than others. I’m not sure I’m answering that the right way, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually confused?\n\nPatient: Unusually confused… I don’t think so, no. I mean, at my age I’ll forget a word or walk into a room and wonder what I came for, but I don’t think I’ve been confused in a way that worried anybody. At least, not that I’ve noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean, doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: Seen things? No, I don’t believe so, doctor. I haven’t seen anyone standing there or anything like that.\n\nSometimes, though… well, I don’t know if this counts. In the quiet afternoons, I’ll hear my old friend call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs, clear as if he were right there. But seeing things, no… not that I can say. Is that alright to mention?\n\nDoctor: During the times when these messages or voices happen, have you also had a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or empty most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I could say that, doctor. When those voices happen, I might feel a bit lonely or low, but… two whole weeks, most of the day nearly every day, down or empty? I don’t think I’ve noticed it like that. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Before these experiences began, had you started any new medicine or begun drinking more alcohol or using any drugs?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. I haven’t noticed anything like starting a new pill or drinking more than usual before it began. And I don’t use drugs. At least… nothing comes to mind that would explain it that way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that you seem less expressive or show less emotion on your face than you used to?\n\nPatient: Well… yes, I suppose a few folks have said I seem quieter, maybe not showing much on my face. I don’t mean to be cold or anything—I still love my family very much.\n\nIt’s just that some days I sit at the kitchen table and don’t say much. The garden will need tending, or choir practice comes and goes, and I just… don’t feel much like getting up and moving. I hope that makes sense, doctor.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any major medical illness around the time this started, such as a serious infection, head injury, or another new health problem?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, not that I can think of. I mean, I’ve had the usual aches and things at my age, but I don’t recall any serious infection or head injury or new illness right around then. I hope I’m answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Are the voice experiences only of your friend who passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly… it’s my old friend who passed away. Some afternoons, when the house is real quiet, I’ll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of the choir songs. It sounds clear, like he’s standing right there.\n\nI know that may sound strange, doctor. I don’t want to make too much of it, but that’s how it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe someone is trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Well… I wouldn’t say someone is out to hurt me exactly, doctor. I don’t want to sound foolish.\n\nBut I did come because sometimes the men at the domino table, and even the radio announcer now and then, say things that feel like little messages meant for me. My wife tells me it’s just ordinary talk, and maybe I shouldn’t make too much of it… but I feel like they’re warning me to be careful.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if your thoughts were being put into your mind or taken out by someone else?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like somebody putting thoughts in my head or taking them out, doctor. Not that I can tell.\n\nIt’s more… well, like I was saying, sometimes the men at the domino table, or the radio announcer, say things that feel like little messages meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe she’s right, but it feels like they’re warning me to be careful. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anything impossible is happening to you, like someone controlling you from a distance or using special powers on you?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, I wouldn’t say somebody is controlling me from far away or using powers on me. That sounds… bigger than what I mean.\n\nIt’s more like what I was trying to say before—the men at the domino table, and sometimes the radio announcer, they’ll say little things that feel meant for me. My wife says it’s just talk, and maybe I shouldn’t carry on about it, but I do feel like they’re warning me to be careful. I hope I’m explaining that all right.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech has become hard to follow or jumbled?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, doctor. Nobody’s really told me I’m jumbled or hard to follow.\n\nSometimes I may ramble a little, but I try to make myself clear. If I’m not answering right, you can tell me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
